[2026-05-18]  v0.9.549: Audit #1 Slice 6e/6f — Variant Imprints (companion-influenced ability upgrades)
The long-term direction from the audit memo: each rank-up choice expands
from (+1 Card / +Damage) to a third (+Imprint) option when a companion is
active. The companion's signature trait is imprinted onto the ability as
a permanent passive rider, stackable up to the ability's rank cap.

Account-level persistence (survives permadeath, mirrors mastery_records).
Spatial gate (companion active at rank-up time) makes companion choice
meaningful at every rank-up. Multi-character stacking — a Mage who grinds
Magic Bolt across many companions ends up with a uniquely flavored ability.

10 trait categories cover all 53 companion types via active.effect mapping:
- Predator's Mark (bonus_damage)
- Hunter's Eye (crit)
- Rending (bleed)
- Toxic Strike (poison)
- Stagger (stun)
- Distract (enemy_miss)
- Bloodletter (lifesteal)
- Soul Tax (mana_drain)
- Mesmerize (charm)
- Aegis (absorb)

Per-stack magnitudes are small (+2%, +1 damage, etc.). Max 4 stacks per
ability. Flavor + customization, not power spike. Central rider
application fires after every damage ability resolves.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

[2026-05-18]  v0.9.548: Audit #12 Slice 11 (Obelisk + Sundial) + Audit #11 Slice 13 (Severe Threat)
Audit #12 — cosmetic catalogue 23 → 25. Obelisk fills the skill-20 gap
(granite memorial pillar, blocking); Sundial fills skill-24 (bronze gnomon
on stone disc, walkable — second floor decoration after Mosaic).

Audit #11 — severe threat downgrade. When 2+ T2+ active dungeons share the
80-tile threat corridor around a post, the post is "severely threatened":
market markup escalates 1.20× → 1.50×, service prices escalate 1.50× →
2.00×, and player-post bubble suppression doubles (-1 → -2). HUD shows a
red ⚠⚠ banner with the dungeon count. Spatial gate (count of active
dungeons), no wall-clock per [[no-real-time-gates]] — clearing one
dungeon immediately steps the post back to normal threat.

------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-18]  v0.9.547: Audit #4 Slice 3.8 — Rename Success to Quality Rating
Playtest feedback: "Success: 31% — does that mean I can fail?" No,
crafts can't fail. The label was misleading legacy wording.

The metric the recipe panel + post-craft panel both display is just
the roll pivot that controls quality band sizes — higher number =
more of the 0-99 roll range falls into Fine/Masterwork. There is no
fail outcome: even a Quality Rating of 5% still rolls Poor at worst,
which produces a 50%-stats item.

Changes:
- crafting_panel.gd: "Success: X%" → "Quality Rating: X%" + a one-line
  reassurance directly under the stat row.
- craft_reveal_panel.gd: "Effective success chance" / "X% success" →
  "Quality Rating X%". Reveal Impact section uses the new label.
  Footer note explains the never-fail invariant.
- show_help / display_changelog updated with the new terminology and a
  callout that Tempered IS a separate gambling mechanic that CAN
  consume materials (kept for honesty; not renamed).

Client-only release — no server change.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-18]  v0.9.546: Audit #4 Slice 3.7 — Reveal impact + recipe preview alignment
Playtest feedback round 3: "Standard reveal gave +15% but my recipe's
base already showed 46% — what did it actually affect?" Root cause:
the recipe preview used the legacy 50-base success_chance formula, but
the actual scratch-off path starts from a 35-base floor. A Standard
reveal landed at exactly the same 46% the preview promised, so it
looked like a no-op.

Fixes:
- handle_craft_list now computes the recipe preview with score=0 (or
  score=3 for auto-skip recipes) so the displayed % matches the floor
  the scratch-off path actually starts from. Reveals genuinely move
  the number now.
- _finalize_craft stamps a baseline_distribution (what bands would be
  at score=0) onto craft_result alongside the effective distribution.
- The panel's 'How the Roll Worked' section now renders a Reveal
  Impact sub-section with the before/after band comparison and a 'Net
  effect: MW +X%, Poor -X% of the roll range' summary line — directly
  answers "what did my +15% actually do?"

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.540: Audit #14 — Friend list (focused project #4)
Account-level friend graph with request/accept consent, plus a block list
that silently drops whispers from blocked users. All state persists across
permadeath via accounts_data.accounts[account_id] additions:
  friends, friend_requests_incoming, friend_requests_outgoing, blocked.

Persistence API (persistence_manager.gd):
- _ensure_social_arrays(account_id) lazy-initializes the four arrays on
  legacy accounts so the schema migration is implicit.
- get_account_id_by_username(username) — case-insensitive resolver
  using the existing username_to_id index.
- send_friend_request: validates not-self / accounts-exist / not-already-
  friends / neither-blocked / not-pending. Mutual outgoing pairs
  auto-accept on the second call.
- accept_friend_request / reject_friend_request / cancel_outgoing_request
  / remove_friend — all idempotent, all keep mirror state consistent on
  both sides.
- block_account also strips friendship + cancels pending requests in BOTH
  directions so post-block state is consistent.
- unblock_account / is_friend / is_blocked / get_friends /
  get_friend_requests_incoming / get_friend_requests_outgoing / get_blocked.
- All mutating ops call save_accounts() immediately.

Server handlers (server.gd):
- handle_friend_add / accept / reject / cancel / remove with shared
  _resolve_friend_target(peer_id, username) helper that surfaces the
  client-facing error if the username doesn't resolve.
- handle_friend_list returns entries[] stamped with online status +
  current character info (character_name / level / class / afk) for
  online friends; offline friends just carry username. Same online_account_ids
  pattern used by clan_list_result.
- handle_friend_requests returns {incoming[], outgoing[]} with usernames.
- handle_block_user / unblock_user / block_list.
- New _peer_id_for_account(account_id) helper used to deliver real-time
  request notifications to the target if they're online.
- handle_private_message: if recipient has blocked sender's account,
  drop the target delivery silently (sender still sees their own echo —
  opaque block, attacker can't probe whether they're blocked vs ignored).

Client commands (client.gd):
- /friend <sub> sub-command suite: list, requests, add, accept, reject,
  cancel, remove. Empty/missing sub-arg defaults to "list".
- /friends = alias for "/friend list". /freq = alias for "/friend requests".
- /block, /unblock, /blocklist (alias /blocked).
- friend_list_result, friend_requests_result, block_list_result render
  handlers using the same compact roster style as /clist (green ● online,
  orange ● AFK, gray ○ offline; character name + Lv + class on online rows).

Closes the full social surface: chat → presence → trade history →
friend graph → block list. Audit #14 progress: ~99% → ~100%.

Files: VERSION.txt, client/client.gd, client/help_panel.gd,
server/server.gd, server/persistence_manager.gd.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.539: Audit #14 — Trade history (focused project #3)
Account-level rolling trade log that survives permadeath. New
/trades [N] chat command (default 10, max 50). Three event kinds:

- direct_trade: counts of items/companions/eggs given+received, plus
  partner character name.
- market_buy: item name, tier, quantity, final price (post-markup),
  seller name (resolved from account if known).
- market_sale: item name, tier, quantity, price, buyer name. Logged
  to seller's account at purchase time so it's visible whenever they
  next /trades — even if they were offline when the sale happened.

Persistence layer (persistence_manager.gd):
- TRADE_HISTORY_CAP = 50
- add_trade_history_entry(account_id, entry) — push_front + trim to
  cap, auto-timestamps, immediate save.
- get_trade_history(account_id, limit) — newest-first deep-copy.
- get_username_for_account(account_id) — resolver for the seller-name
  lookup when the seller is offline.

Server hooks:
- handle_market_buy logs to buyer_account_id AND seller_account_id at
  the post-save point, before market_buy_success is sent.
- The direct trade finalize block (next to the trade_complete pushes)
  logs one entry per side using char_a/char_b names.
- New trade_history message type → handle_trade_history → returns
  trade_history_result with entries[] + limit.

Client:
- /trades, /tradehistory commands + keyword whitelist.
- trade_history_result handler formats each entry by kind with date
  (Time.get_datetime_dict_from_unix_time), event tag color, counterparty,
  and amount. Empty case shows a guidance hint.
- show_help RECENT ADDITIONS extended with the /trades bullet.

Audit #14 progress: ~98% → ~99%. **Focused project #3 landed.**

Files: VERSION.txt, client/client.gd, server/server.gd,
server/persistence_manager.gd.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.537: Audit #14 — Functional mentor reward bonus
Mentor + mentee party composition (Lv 20+ /mentor on partied with a
Lv < 10 player) grants +25% XP to every surviving party member on
combat victory. Until v0.9.537, /mentor on was a purely cosmetic ★
badge; this turns it into a real gameplay incentive.

Implementation:
- combat_manager.gd
  - New _has_mentor_mentee_pair(combat) helper iterates combat.members
    skipping dead, checks each character for (level >= 20 && mentor_active)
    and (level < 10). Returns true when both flags present on DIFFERENT
    characters (a single character can't be both — Lv ranges don't overlap).
  - _process_party_victory checks the helper once before the per-member
    loop; if true, applies mentor_mult = 1.25 inside the same XP product
    as house_xp_mult + level-gap scaling. Adds a "✦ Mentor bonus: +25% XP
    for the party!" line to the reward messages.

Why this matters:
- Mentors get a concrete reason to party low-level players (not just
  the social badge).
- Mentees get a meaningful XP boost during their most fragile levels.
- The whole /mentor + /mentors + ★ + [NEW Lv X] stack from v0.9.517-521
  now coordinates real stakes, not pure flavor.

Tuning: 1.25× chosen to be impactful but not overpowered. Folds
multiplicatively with house bonuses and level-gap modifier so the
high-end ceiling stays under control.

Audit #14 progress: ~95% → ~98%.

Files: VERSION.txt, client/client.gd, client/help_panel.gd,
shared/combat_manager.gd.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.530: Audit #14 — Party chat (/p) + clan roster online indicator

  time the player starts any gather session, a modal explains the

  explaining the seven slots (Weapon/Armor/Helm/Shield/Boots/Ring/Amulet),
  swap behaviour, comparison view, tier + variant colors, the Salvage
  flow for low-tier gear, and Home Stone (Equipment) permadeath
  protection.

handle_fish_start, handle_mine_start, handle_log_start, and
handle_inventory_equip. Both flags persist via to_dict / from_dict and
default false for legacy characters.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.526: Narrow v0.9.525 over-rip — restore world-state systems
Per user direction: the no-real-time-gates rule is specifically about
PLAYER-FACING repeatable-quest cooldowns ("wait 24h to redo this"),
NOT about world-state mechanics like resource regrowth, structure decay,
or vendor rotation. v0.9.525 over-corrected.

Memo updated: feedback_no_real_time_gates.md narrowed to clarify scope.

Kept ripped (player-facing cooldowns — still bad):
- Repeatable chain 24h cooldown (T1/T2/T3 chains stay immediately repeatable).
- Daily quest 24h cooldown (Character.can_accept_daily_quest still true).

Restored (world-state — fine):
- Wall decay 72h grace (_tick_wall_decay re-enabled).
- Node respawn: water 5min + near-post non-water 4min (chunk_manager.deplete_node).
- Guard food upkeep (_tick_guard_decay re-enabled).
- Post inactivity decay (_compute_post_inactivity_state restored).
- NPC vendor daily stock rotation (get_npc_daily_stock seeds with day_index again).
- Dynamic quest board date seeding (_get_date_string returns YYYYMMDD).

Post inactivity thresholds bumped per user feedback:
- POST_INACTIVE_THRESHOLD_DAYS: 7 → 30
- POST_ABANDONED_THRESHOLD_DAYS: 30 → 90
Life gets busy — a couple weeks off shouldn't tag a post.
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-17]  v0.9.525: Rip all real-world time gates
Per user direction 2026-05-18: real-world clock cooldowns/timers/decay
are bad for this game. Use gameplay-driven gates instead. Saved as
feedback_no_real_time_gates.md workflow rule.

This release rips every wall-clock-dependent mechanic the codebase
had accumulated. Each system stays functional but loses its real-time
gate; balance can be re-tuned later via gameplay-driven replacements
(step / kill / completion / currency gates) if needed.

Ripped:
1. Repeatable chain 24h cooldown (v0.9.517/522/524 — ripped). Chains
   marked repeatable reappear immediately after completion.
2. Daily quest 24h cooldown (Character.can_accept_daily_quest). Daily
   quests can be re-accepted immediately.
3. Wall decay 72h grace (server _tick_wall_decay loop disabled). Walls
   never crumble — un-enclosed or otherwise.
4. Node respawn time (chunk_manager.deplete_node):
   - Water nodes: never deplete (infinite fishing).
   - All non-water nodes: DEPLETED_PERMANENT (consistent with prior
     far-from-post behaviour). The 4-min near-post respawn is gone.
5. Guard food decay (server _tick_guard_decay → no-op). Guards no
   longer leave from running out of food.
6. Post inactivity decay (server _compute_post_inactivity_state → always
   "active"). Posts no longer tag as Inactive (7d) or Abandoned (30d).
7. NPC vendor daily stock rotation (trading_post_database.get_npc_daily_stock).
   Seed now post_id + category only; each post has stable stock forever.
   Different posts still carry different goods — variety preserved.
8. Dynamic quest board date rotation (quest_database._get_date_string).
   Returns a static string; quest content at each board index is stable.
   The regenerating slide-forward window (Slice 13 v0.9.453) still keeps
   the board fresh as quests complete.

The shutdown sentinel (60s countdown for server restart) stays — it's
infrastructure, not gameplay.

Field cleanup: character.chain_cooldowns and daily_quest_cooldowns
remain in the schema for back-compat with legacy save data but are
no longer read.
------------------------------------------------------------

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.513: Apex Variants — the apex frontier gets teeth
Four-slice batch building on v0.9.512's geometric apex frontier zone.

**Slice 1 — Apex Variants:**
Every monster spawned in the apex frontier zone (distance > 1500 from origin) is now an "Apex Variant":
- "Apex" prefix added to display name ("Apex Goblin", "Apex Wraith")
- +25% HP, +10% damage
- Purple name color (#9F70FF) in combat
- `is_apex_variant` flag stamped on monster dict

Doesn't apply in dungeon combat — dungeons have their own tier scaling. Logic lives in `trigger_flock_encounter`. combat_manager now reads `monster.name_color` for the name-color override (apex purple) before falling back to class-affinity color.

**Slice 2 — Bonus XP on apex kills:**
- v0.9.512 zone bonus: +10% XP (unchanged)
- NEW variant bonus: +20% XP on top, stacking → +30% total per apex variant kill
- Reward message names "Apex Variant Bonus" vs "Apex Frontier Bonus" depending on which flags are set.

Pays for the +25% HP the variant carries — without the variant XP bump, apex kills would be XP/effort negative.

**Slice 3 — Bonus Soul Gems on apex kills:**
`roll_monster_gem_drop` now multiplies gem_count by 1.50 when `monster.is_apex_variant` is true. Pairs with the XP bonus to make apex content meaningfully rewarding.

**Slice 4 — First-time apex frontier tutorial overlay:**
New `seen_apex_frontier_hint` per-character flag (export, to_dict, from_dict). When the player first crosses into the apex zone, server fires a `tutorial_hint` modal explaining the +XP / +gems / variant mechanics. Lives in `_check_apex_frontier_entry` (called inline from `send_location_update`'s apex flag derivation) so it fires exactly when the border is crossed.

Continues closing Audit #10's "apex content" captured item — future beats can stack named extreme zones, unique drops, or T9 encounter pools on top.
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-17]  v0.9.512: Apex Frontier first beat + Legacy variant reroll fix
Two-slice batch.

**Slice 1 — Apex Frontier (Audit #10 first beat):**
The far edges of the world now reward exploration. Distance > 1500 tiles from origin is an "apex frontier" zone. Monsters killed there grant +10% XP. Visual marker on the Area line in the region label, and the combat reward message names the bonus on kill.

- shared/world_system.gd: new `APEX_FRONTIER_DISTANCE = 1500` const + `is_apex_frontier(x, y)` helper (squared-distance check for efficiency).
- server/server.gd: `send_location_update` includes `is_apex_frontier`; `trigger_flock_encounter` stamps the flag on the monster dict at engagement time.
- shared/combat_manager.gd: `_process_victory_with_abilities` reads `monster.is_apex_frontier`, applies 1.10× to final_xp, appends purple "⚡ Apex Frontier Bonus: +10% XP!" message alongside the existing hotspot bonus message.
- client/client.gd: `hud_is_apex_frontier` flag from location updates; `update_region_label` appends "[⚡ APEX +10% XP]" tag to the Area line in purple (#9F70FF) so it's visually distinct from the orange DANGER hotspot tag.

First beat of "apex content" captured item. Future slices can stack T9 encounter pools, unique drops, or named extreme zones on top of this geometric definition.

**Slice 2 — Legacy character appearance variant reroll fix:**
Characters created BEFORE the appearance variant system had `appearance_variant = ""` on disk. `Character.from_dict()` rolled a fresh non-deterministic variant on every call, causing the same character to render with different colors on different UI surfaces in the same session.

Fixed by seeding the reroll with `hash(character_name)`:
- shared/drop_tables.gd: new `_roll_egg_variant_with_rng(rng)` helper accepting a caller-supplied RandomNumberGenerator.
- shared/character.gd: from_dict's legacy migration branch creates an RNG seeded with `hash(name)` and calls the new helper.

Closes the [[project-legacy-variant-reroll]] tracking memo. Shared scripts touched → server deploy required.
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-17]  v0.9.511: Niche-passive surface extended to Bestiary + Victory card
Two-slice batch. Client-only — no server changes.

Continues v0.9.510's Audit #2 closure by extending the niche-passive surface (Paladin Divine Favor / Ranger Hunter's Mark) to two more places the player sees enemies:

**Slice 1 — Bestiary niche-passive icon:**
Each entry's name gets a ✦ icon prefix when the player's class passive applies (gold ✦ Paladin → undead/demons, green ✦ Ranger → beasts). Summary header gains a legend explaining the icon. Lets a Paladin/Ranger scan their kill ledger and see which species their +25% damage targets.

- New _get_niche_passive_prefix helper in bestiary_panel.gd using CombatManager._monster_matches_keywords + _UNDEAD_DEMON_KEYWORDS / _BEAST_KEYWORDS for 1:1 parity with combat-time damage calc.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.509: Owner-post listing bonus
Closes Audit #11 captured item "Owner-discount listings — Player-only."

When a seller lists an item (single or bulk) at a trading post their account owns (player post id format `player_<account_username>_<index>`), an extra +25% valor is credited directly to the seller's balance.

Design: the listing's base_valor stays at the standard calculation (Halfling + Knight bonuses only) — buyers pay the same price they would at an NPC post. The +25% is a separate top-up paid directly to the seller's valor, not absorbed into the listing. So owner posts don't become more expensive to shop at; they just pay their owner more to list there.

Three call sites updated:
- handle_market_list_item (single listing) — adds owner_bonus_amount, credits to seller, sends chat-line acknowledgement.
- handle_market_list_all (bulk listing) — applies bonus once on aggregated total at the end; per-listing base_valor unchanged.
- handle_market_list_preview (preview) — shows owner_bonus_amount in the confirm dialog so the seller sees the boosted total before committing.

Market Help topic updated to explain the bonus.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.502: HP estimates bias upward to stop "1/x HP but won't die"
Second half of the 2026-05-17 combat readability ask. Cross-level HP
estimates now over-estimate slightly so the displayed bar drains
toward true HP from ABOVE, not from below.

## Root cause

The client's `estimate_enemy_hp()` extrapolated across levels using
only the base `_calculate_tiered_stat_scale` curve. But the server's
actual HP calc in `monster_database.gd` layers a hyperbolic
`hp_multiplier` on top (2.0× → 7.0× asymptote, keyed on expected
player gear at the target level). So when the player had killed a
Lv 5 monster and met a Lv 10 of the same type, the client estimate
could be 281 HP while actual was 330. The bar would drain to 0
with the monster still alive — looked bugged.

## Fix

Cross-level estimates are now padded upward by 4% per level of
gap, capped at +50%. Same-level data (`known_hp` = damage dealt
in last kill, by construction ≥ actual HP) is still returned
unpadded — it's already a safe upper bound.

As the player accumulates same-level kills, the system narrows
toward truth from above rather than guessing low. Pad is invisible
to the player; the felt experience is "this monster has more HP
than I thought" rather than "why isn't this dying."

Closes [[combat-readability-2026-05-17]] slice 2/2. Pairs with
v0.9.501 (longer damage popups + animated HP drain).

Client-only.

Files: VERSION.txt, client/client.gd

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-17]  v0.9.501: Combat readability — longer damage popups + animated HP drain
First half of the combat readability follow-up raised 2026-05-17.

## What ships

1. Floating damage numbers stay readable ~3× longer.
   - _spawn_damage_label linger 0.65s → 1.95s, fade 0.25s → 0.75s.
     Total dwell: 0.9s → 2.7s.
   - show_dot_tick lifetime 0.85s → 2.55s (DoT/proc ticks).
   - DAMAGE_STACK_RESET_S 1.5s → 3.0s so rapid hits within the new
     linger still stack up the screen instead of overlapping the
     still-visible previous popup.

2. HP bars drain smoothly over 1 second instead of snapping.
   - New _animate_bar_value(bar, target, dur=1.0) helper. Kills any
     in-progress tween on the bar (rapid hits don't queue stale
     tweens). Uses TRANS_QUAD + EASE_OUT for a satisfying drop.
   - Applied to _refresh_player_hp, _refresh_monster_hp,
     update_companion_combat_hp, and the battlefield overlay
     (_overlay_player_hp_bar + _overlay_companion_hp_bar).
   - Lufia mirror bars get the same animation.
   - Initial population at combat start snaps the bar (no drain from
     0 → max). populate() drops the per-bar "hp_drain_initialized"
     meta so a fresh combat starts clean.
   - Numeric labels (e.g., "HP 84 / 150") still update instantly —
     the number is the truth, the bar is the dramatic reveal.

The third combat-readability ask (monster HP estimates should
over-estimate, not undershoot — see project_combat_readability_2026_05_17.md)
is queued for v0.9.502.

Client-only.

Files: VERSION.txt, client/client.gd, client/combat_scene_panel.gd

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.496: Audit #4 Slice 1B — Tier Ascension Fusion
Adds a 4th fusion mode at the Companion Stable's Fuse tab. Select 3
companions of the SAME monster_type AND the SAME tier (any sub_tier
mix) and consume 1 Ascension Catalyst to produce 1 companion of that
same monster_type at tier+1, sub_tier 1. Lower-tier companions are no
longer relegated to fusion fodder — a Tier 1 Goblin can be ascended all
the way to Tier 9 while keeping its identity, art, name, and ability
lookup. Variant inherits if all 3 parents share it; otherwise it rolls
fresh. Tier 9 is the cap. Inputs come from kennel or registered slots;
the slot-preserving rule still applies (any registered input → output
is auto-registered).

New consumable: Ascension Catalyst. T6+ dungeon chest drop, weights
T6=1, T7=2, T8=2, T9=3. Rarer than Hybrid Catalysts since a single use
yields a much larger payoff (5-step tier climb vs Hybrid's single
capstone moment).

Server: handle_stable_fusion + handle_house_fusion both grow an
"ascend" branch (same validation pattern as hybrid — exact-type check,
exact-tier check, tier<9 check, catalyst consumption). New
drop_tables.create_ascended_companion helper. _build_companion_stable_payload
surfaces ascension_catalyst_count alongside hybrid_catalyst_count.

Client: companion_stable_panel grows FUSE_ASCEND constant +
FUSE_MODE_RULES entry + a 4th mode button. Mode-specific hint,
empty-state message, candidate filter (tier<9), and selection preview
all added. admin_panel grows "Give 3x Hybrid Catalyst" and
"Give 3x Ascension Catalyst" buttons under Items page; client wires
both to gm_giveconsumable.

Help text: companion_stable + fusion_overview topics updated to four
modes; new Ascend description line includes catalyst rarity. Changelog
in client.gd updated; oldest entry (v0.9.481) dropped per the 5-version
rule.

Admin shorthands: /giveconsumable accepts hybcat / hybrid / asccat /
ascend for symmetry with existing home-stone shorthands.

CLAUDE.md: captures the durable "Admin Commands: /admin UI, Not Chat"
rule (UI control over chat controls) saved during the v0.9.490
session.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.495: Mixed T9 fusion requires T8.8 specifically (Tier 8, sub-tier 8)
Existing validation only checked sub_tier == 8, which let lower-tier
sub_tier 8 companions through (e.g. T1.8 Goblins → random T9). The
intent is that Mixed T9 is the capstone fusion — only fully-maxed Tier 8
companions count as inputs. Description never mentioned the tier
requirement either.

Tightens validation across both fusion paths AND clarifies all surfaces:

- server.gd handle_stable_fusion mixed branch: tier == 8 AND sub_tier == 8
- server.gd handle_house_fusion mixed branch (Sanctuary F-tile): same
- client.gd _count_t8_companions + _get_t8_companions: tier == 8 filter
- companion_stable_panel.gd _candidate_matches_mode: mixed mode rejects
  non-tier-8 candidates; hint, empty-state, and validation preview all
  say "T8.8 (Tier 8, sub-tier 8)" explicitly
- help_panel.gd companion_stable + fusion_overview topics: rewritten to
  say T8.8 instead of just "sub-tier 8"

Balance note: practical impact is minimal — players don't grind sub_tier 8
of low-tier monsters (3^7 = 2187 wild eggs per sub_tier 8). Anyone who'd
done Mixed T9 was already feeding T8.8s. The fix removes a latent
exploit and aligns description with intent.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-17]  v0.9.494: Mixed T9 + Hybrid modes in the Stable's Fuse tab
v0.9.489 shipped Same Type fusion at the Stable. The server-side
handle_stable_fusion was already generic — it accepted "mixed" and
"hybrid" fusion types but the panel only exposed Same. This wires up the
remaining two modes via a mode selector above the candidate list.

Modes:
- Same Type: 3 of same monster type AND sub-tier → next sub-tier (cap 8)
- Mixed T9: 8 companions all at sub-tier 8 → random Tier 9
- Hybrid: 2 of DIFFERENT monster types, both sub-tier 5+, consumes 1
  Hybrid Catalyst → hybrid blend

Mode-specific behavior:
- Candidate filter: Same shows all; Mixed shows only sub_tier 8; Hybrid
  shows only sub_tier 5+
- Selection cap: 3 / 8 / 2 per mode
- Validation preview line names the exact requirement that's failing
  ("All 8 must be at sub-tier 8.", "Hybrid requires DIFFERENT monster
  types.", etc.)
- Hybrid: catalyst availability shown in hint, gate the fuse button

All inputs can be kennel or registered. If any input is registered, the
output is auto-registered (slot-preserving) per the v0.9.489 inheritance
logic. Auto-checkout (v0.9.492) and active-companion-fuseable (v0.9.491)
behaviors apply equally across all three modes.

Server changes:
- _build_companion_stable_payload includes hybrid_catalyst_count (summed
  across all hybrid_catalyst stacks in character.inventory)

Client changes:
- companion_stable_panel.gd grows _current_fuse_mode + FUSE_MODE_RULES
  dict; selector buttons; mode-aware filter / cap / validation preview;
  separate guidance text per mode
- HelpPanel companion_stable topic rewritten to describe all 3 modes

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.493: Check Out registered companions mid-character at the Stable
Previously the only way to pull a registered companion onto your active
character was the login-time path (Char Select → Sanctuary → enter game).
A stuck companion in a registered slot meant logging out — and there was
no live recovery option mid-character.

Companion Stable's Manage tab. Each registered slot shows as a compact
card with monster name, slot number, level, and a "← Check Out" button.

  the player has NO current active companion (deposit first if you do),
  that the slot has a companion AND it isn't already checked out by
  another character. Hands off to the existing
  _checkout_companion_for_character helper which handles the kennel

  Sanctuary slot N. Now your active companion.") and refreshes the panel
  + house data.

fuse + check-out are all available at any T5+ Companion Stable.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.492: Fusion output auto-checks-out when active was consumed
When you fuse your active registered companion (v0.9.491 enabled this),
the output gets auto-registered to a slot — but the player's character
was left with no active companion until they manually withdrew/checked
out the result.

Now: if the active checkout slot was among the consumed inputs AND the
output is auto-registered (was_registered_fusion), the server auto-
checks-out the output as the new active companion. Preserves continuity
— "I fused my pet" → "I still have an active pet, now upgraded."

Success message gets a green "→ Now your active companion." tail so the
player knows.

Edge: if the registered slots are unexpectedly full and the output falls
back to the kennel, no auto-checkout (kennel companions don't have the
same slot semantics). Player would manually withdraw at the Stable.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.491: Active registered companion now appears in Fuse list
Previously the Fuse tab filtered out registered slots where checked_out_by
was set — i.e., the player's currently-active registered companion was
invisible as a fusion input. Required a Manage → Return to Slot detour to
get it into the list.

Now the active registered companion shows in the Fuse tab with [REG]
[ACTIVE] markers and is directly fuseable. When consumed, the server
clears character.active_companion, using_registered_companion, and
registered_companion_slot, plus strips the mirrored entry from
collected_companions (the checkout path adds it there for combat use).

The output still auto-registers if any input was registered (existing
v0.9.489 inheritance logic) — including the active checkout slot. So
"fuse my main pet with 2 others" works in one step: select all three on
the Fuse tab, press Fuse, the output appears in the registered list, you
withdraw/check out the result to use it again.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.488: Companion Stable deposit no longer touches registration
v0.9.487's auto-unregister on registered deposit was conflating two separate
operations: transport (Stable) and registration status. Deposit and
registration are independent — depositing a registered companion should
return it to its registered slot, preserving the death-insurance status.

Changes:
- handle_companion_stable_deposit: registered active → return_companion_to_house
  (writes current state back to the slot). Non-registered → kennel as before.
- Companion Stable panel: registered deposit shows "→ Return to Slot" with
  tooltip explaining registration is preserved.
- Tutorial body + help topic rewritten to make the two paths explicit.

Fusion still operates on kennel inputs only; mid-character fusion access
(with registered inputs + slot-preserving output) lands in v0.9.489.

[2026-05-17]  v0.9.487: Hotfix — Companion Stable z-order, map shrink, registered fusion
Three fixes from v0.9.485 playtest:

1. First-time tutorial overlay was rendering BEHIND the Companion Stable
   panel — both arrive in the same message burst (companion_stable_open +
   tutorial_hint), and the Stable panel was added to the scene tree later
   so it drew on top. Fix: TutorialHintPanel.show_hint() now calls
   move_to_front() before becoming visible. Also added top_level=true so
   tree position no longer affects render order.

2. ASCII map shrank after first Companion Stable open (user had to reset
   via the UI Scale menu). Root cause: nested CenterContainer +
   PRESET_FULL_RECT inside the panels was perturbing sibling layout even
   while hidden. Fix: top_level=true on CompanionStablePanel and HelpPanel
   so they're true viewport-anchored modals and ignore parent transform.

3. Registered companions could not be deposited — but registered
   companions are exactly the ones players want to fuse. Fix: deposit now
   auto-unregisters when the active companion has using_registered=true.
   Tooltip + tutorial body explain the consequence ("frees the registered
   slot; re-register the fused result via Home Stone (Companion) later").
   A [REGISTERED] marker is shown alongside [ACTIVE] in the panel so the
   player sees the state before pressing Deposit. Rollback path restores
   the registered slot if the kennel was full.

[2026-05-16]  v0.9.484: Audit #4 Slice 4 — Mixed-Type Hybrid Fusion v1
Closes the audit's "Fusion OVERHAUL — add mixed-type fusion that combines
abilities" decision. Largest still-open companion lever (#4 was at ~50%).

New "Hybrid" tab on the Fusion Station alongside Same Type and Mixed T9.
Pick 2 companions of DIFFERENT monster_types, both at sub_tier >= 5, plus
1 Hybrid Catalyst in inventory. Produces a hybrid that:

- Keeps parent A's monster_type (drives base ability lookup + sprite/art)
- Stores parent B's type as `hybrid_partner_type` for ability blending
- Bonuses dict = numeric average of both parents + 10% hybrid vigor
- Tier = max(A.tier, B.tier); sub_tier resets to 1 (own ladder)
- Variant inherits if both parents share one, else rolls fresh
- Name displays "Hybrid <A>-<B>" so both lineages are visible

Ability blending (extends get_monster_companion_abilities w/ optional
hybrid_partner_type arg, threaded through 3 call sites in character.gd +
combat_manager.gd):

- Passive: static "Hybrid Vigor" (+5% damage, no level gate)
- Active: parent A's active (signature ability stays)
- Threshold: parent B's threshold (at level 15+)

Hybrid Catalyst: new consumable item, T5+ dungeon chest drop. Weights:
T5=1, T6=2, T7=2, T8=2, T9=3 (slow but steady late-game supply). Marked
as consumable in _generate_item, name "Hybrid Catalyst" in
_get_tiered_consumable_name, no tier prefix.

Server validates type-differ + sub_tier >= 5 + catalyst possession,
consumes 1 catalyst from inventory (mirrors the existing inventory-discard
pattern: decrement quantity, remove stack if empty), calls
create_hybrid_companion, persists via fuse_companions (parents destroyed).

Out of scope (deferred to future slices per design lock):
- Per-pair authored signature hybrid abilities (e.g., Goblin x Wolf =
  unique "Gnoll Tactician" ability) — currently uses parent A's signature
- Visual variant blending (color1 from A + color2 from B)
- 3-way hybrids; catalyst crafting recipe

v0.9.481 pinned equipment correctly into inventory but the victory card's

------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-16]  v0.9.481: Early-game survivability — threat-corridor stat downscale + pinned equipment
Two fixes for the early-game lethality + gearing complaints surfacing after
v0.9.479/v0.9.480 stretched the starter-area curve.

1. monster_database.gd `_calculate_tiered_stat_scale` now applies a linear
   downscale (target / base) when target_level < base_level. v0.9.480 clamped
   the threat-corridor's rolled level but the actual combat stats still used
   the monster's base_level — a Lv 1 spawn of a base-44 Chimaera fought like
   the full T3 Chimaera (~350 HP, ~41 STR). Win rate vs threat-corridor mobs
   in the starter buffer was effectively 0%. Now apex monsters spilling into
   low-level zones collapse to runt-tier stats while keeping name + abilities,
   reading as tier-1-equivalent fights with thematic flavor. Fix at the
   scaling function catches every callsite (threat corridor, forced monsters
   via scrolls, future systems).

2. server.gd `_build_combat_loot_bag` now pins equipment drops outside the
   16-slot random pool. Pre-scratch-off (before v0.9.371), combat equipment
   dropped directly to inventory at ~3.3% per fight. The scratch-off swallowed
   that cadence — equipment landed in a random slot, and at T1 with 1 reveal
   the effective rate dropped to ~0.2% per fight. Equipment is now auto-
   awarded on bag creation and surfaced in a ✦ Equipment Found ✦ banner above
   the grid (combat_loot_panel.gd). Scratch-off remains pure bonus content
   (eggs / mats / parts / tools / consumables / currency / filler). Source
   drop rate unchanged.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-15]  v0.9.480: Hotfix — starter buffer + threat-corridor clamp
Two layered bugs broke v0.9.479's starter buffer:

1. Post-anchored level used a LINEAR blend between the two nearest
   trading posts. Procedural posts are forced 70+ tiles from haven
   (min spacing), so the second-nearest post anchors at Lv 10+.
   Linear lerp dragged the area level near haven up to Lv 4-5 at
   just 30 tiles out — Area Lv HUD reported "Lv 4" right outside
   the starter post even though wilderness curve said Lv 2.

   Fix: switched the post-blend to a cubic (t^3) curve so each
   post DOMINATES its immediate vicinity. Haven's Lv 1 anchor now
   stays in control through the 30-tile buffer and only ramps
   toward the next post when the player is genuinely close to it.
   Mid-game zones are unaffected because the wilderness floor
   dominates there anyway.

2. The Audit #11 Slice 9 threat-corridor bias (T2+ active dungeons
   spilling their monster_type into surrounding world) was rolling
   levels from the DUNGEON'S min/max, not the area's. A T3 Chimaera
   Gorge within 80 tiles of haven spawned Lv 50+ Chimaeras in the
   Lv 1-2 buffer.

   Fix: clamp rolled_level to level_range.min/max so the threat's
   MONSTER TYPE still spawns (preserves the thematic spill — you
   see Chimaeras where you'd expect Goblins) but at a level the
   player can actually fight. Area Lv HUD now matches encounter.

Server-side fixes only on the gameplay side (world_system.gd +
server.gd); client gets the changelog entry.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-15]  v0.9.479: Starter-area Lv 1-2 buffer
Players starting at Lv 1 were running into Lv 4-7 monsters as soon as
they stepped outside the starter post — too steep for a fresh character.

Stretched the early _distance_to_level curve so the first ring outside
the safe zone is a true Lv 1-2 buffer:

- d 0-10: Lv 1 (safe zone — unchanged, no spawns)
- d 10-30: Lv 1-2  (NEW novice band — 20-tile ring of Lv 1-2)
- d 30-60: Lv 2-6  (NEW gentle ramp — second ring)
- d 60-150: Lv 6-50  (existing endpoint at d=150 → lv 50 preserved)
- d 150+: unchanged

The d=150 endpoint preservation means the mid- and late-game world
curves are untouched. Only the immediate starter-post vicinity got
softer, plus a smooth ramp back to the original curve by d=150.

Side effect: trading posts at distance 10-30 (east_market d=27,
west_shrine d=27) now anchor at Lv 2 instead of Lv 7, matching their
tier-1 designation. Player-post settler bubbles with T1 effective tier
still resolve to Lv 1 via level_for_tier (TIER_REFERENCE_DISTANCE[1] = 10
which is in the safe zone).

[2026-05-15]  v0.9.474: Audit #3 Slice 3 — first-level-up progression hint
Discoverability hook for the Progression Vectors dashboard (v0.9.437).
The first time a character has unspent stat points (right after their
first level-up under the system), the server sends a one-shot teaching
message pointing at /stats and /status. Fires once per character.

- character.gd: new @export var seen_progression_hint: bool = false.
  Serialized + deserialized in to_dict / from_dict; legacy characters
  default to false so they get the hint on next character_update if
  they already have unspent points.
- server.gd: new _maybe_send_progression_hint(peer_id) helper called
  from send_character_update (cheap: returns immediately once the flag
  is set). Conditions: unspent_stat_points > 0 AND not
  seen_progression_hint. On fire: sets the flag, sends a text message
  with /stats + /status pointers, saves character.

the ongoing visibility surface; this is purely the one-time teaching
moment for new characters.

------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-15]  v0.9.472: Audit #2 Slice 3 — niche-passive keyword fix + beast expansion
Two issues in the Paladin Divine Favor (+25% vs undead/demons) and Ranger
Hunter's Mark (+25% vs beasts) class passives:

1. Exact-name matching silently failed against rare-variant prefixes.
   Fighting a "Corrosive Skeleton", "Sundering Vampire", or
   "★ Lich Champion" never triggered the passive because the lookup was
   `monster.name.to_lower() in undead_demon_names` — a Corrosive prefix
   broke the match. Same for beasts.

2. The beast list was missing all the dragons (Young/Ancient/Primordial
   Dragon, Dragon Wyrmling), Phoenix, and Sphinx — all beast-flavored
   mythical creatures from T3-T9 that Rangers should naturally benefit
   against.

Fix:
- New static `_monster_matches_keywords(monster, keywords)` helper does
  substring matching against both monster.type and monster.name (both
  lowercased). Variant prefixes are now harmless.
- New const lists _UNDEAD_DEMON_KEYWORDS and _BEAST_KEYWORDS at the top
  of combat_manager.gd. Beast list expanded with "dragon" (catches all
  4 dragon types), "phoenix", "sphinx", "bear" (future-proof).
- Divine Favor and Hunter's Mark both rewritten to use the helper.

Net effect: Paladin and Ranger niche passives are reliably load-bearing
again across T1-T9 content. Audit #2 Slice 3.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-15]  v0.9.471: Audit #12 Slice 5 — abandoned-post bubble decay
Slice 4 (v0.9.460) made post inactivity visible. Slice 5 gives it teeth.

Mechanics:
- Inactive (7-30d): suppression -= 1 (additional erode, stacks with threat)
- Abandoned (30+d): suppression = 0 (full decay; bubble offers no protection)
- Tending (move/build/feed) resets last_tended_at — cheap but real

Server (server.gd):
- _compute_effective_post_tier reads new _compute_post_inactivity_state
  helper and applies the decay erode after the threat erode (same shape
  as Audit #11 Slice 11)
- _compute_post_inactivity_state extracted as a helper (was inline in
  _compute_player_post_status_data); reused by tier compute + status data
- _build_player_post_status (BBCode /post) appends a "Bubble suppression
  weakened by inactivity (-1)" line under Inactive tag, or "FULLY DECAYED
  — no protection until tended" under ABANDONED tag
- _compute_player_post_status_data stamps decay_suppression_state:
  'none' / 'weakened' / 'fully_decayed' for the visual panel

Client (post_status_panel.gd):
- _inactivity_label renders the matching decay cue under the tag (same
  amber / red color pairing as the BBCode path)

Pairs with the threat-suppression decay from v0.9.464: a post that's
both inactive AND threatened compounds the erode. An abandoned post
with no protection is the worst case; tending it costs nothing more
than walking through it.

Audit #12 Slice 5.

[2026-05-15]  v0.9.470: Audit #11 Slice 12 — NPC-post threat -> quest hook
When a trading post is Under Threat (T2+ active dungeon within 80 tiles),
the quest board now injects a tier-scaled DUNGEON_CLEAR bounty pointing at
the threatening dungeon. Gives the existing threat marker an immediate
player-facing reason (there's a paying job here) and stitches the
threat-state system (v0.9.454 / v0.9.464) to the quest board.

Server (server.gd):
- _compute_post_threat_state now includes dungeon_type + instance_id in
  the result so callers can target the specific threat
- New _generate_threat_relief_quest helper builds a DUNGEON_CLEAR quest
  dict with id format threat_<post_id>@<dungeon_type>
- handle_trading_post_quests injects the quest after progression quest
- _THREAT_RELIEF_REWARDS table scales by tier (T2 350xp/50v -> T9
  2700xp/300v) — mirrors chain final-stage scaling, slightly lower
  since this is a single quest, not a chain

Shared (quest_database.gd):
- get_quest routes threat_<...>@<...> IDs to new
  _regenerate_threat_relief_quest regenerator so turn-in works after
  the live threat clears (parses post_id + dungeon_type from the id;
  rewards come from extra_data.stored_rewards on turn-in)

Client (client.gd):
- handle_quest_list sorts threat-relief quests to the top (above
  featured) and surfaces a ⚠ THREAT BOUNTY tag in the dungeon's color

Quest id encodes <post>@<dungeon_type> so the same threat at the same
post is one-and-done per character; a different dungeon_type at the same
post generates a separate bounty. Auto-disappears once the threatening
dungeon clears (threat state empties).

When a T2+ active dungeon is within THREAT_CORRIDOR_RADIUS of a
player post, the post's settler bubble loses 1 effective suppression
(floor at 0) until the threat is cleared. Monsters spawning inside
the bubble go up by 1 tier while threatened.

- _compute_effective_post_tier now reads _compute_post_threat_state
  and applies the -1 suppression when threatened.
- _build_player_post_status appends "Bubble suppression weakened by
  threat (-1)" under the Under Threat line.
- _compute_player_post_status_data stamps threat.suppression_weakened
  for the visual panel.
- post_status_panel.gd renders the suppression-weakened line under
  the threat banner.

Third mechanical bite on Under Threat marker (after +50% services /
+20% market markup from earlier slices, and threat-corridor monster
bias from Slice 9). Pairs the marker with consequence that hits the
player's OWN base.

Bubble cache picks up the change on next guard mutation; immediate
display via post_status panel.

[2026-05-15]  v0.9.455: Audit #9 Slice 5 — Travel Stone / network buy
New consumable: Travel Stone. Spend 1 from inventory to buy a remote
listing via the Network Browse view without traveling. Closes the
audit's final pending slice.

Pricing uses the listing's-post markup only — specialty discount and
threat multiplier are skipped to preserve geographic value (specialty
deals and threat-discounted shopping still require physical presence).

Acquisition paths:
- T5/T6/T7/T8/T9 chest drops at weight 1-4, rare/epic rarity
- Curiosity Trader at exotic posts: 3000 Valor, rare, in the daily
  rotating stock pool from v0.9.451

shared/drop_tables.gd:
- New POTION_EFFECTS["travel_stone"] marker entry (no in-combat effect)
- _get_item_name returns "Travel Stone" for the type
- Added to T5-T9 chest tables at low weight

shared/trading_post_database.gd:
- CATEGORY_STOCK_POOLS["exotic"] gains the Travel Stone entry

server/server.gd:
- New handle_market_network_buy(peer_id, message). Validates: player
  is at any market, has at least one travel_stone, listing exists at
  given post_id (not the current post), can afford the listing's-post
  markup, has inventory space. Atomically: removes listing, spends
  valor, consumes stone (defensive re-scan if index drifted), credits
  seller's base_valor, delivers item, records sale in rolling avg
  history. Refunds on stone-vanish race.

client/client.gd:
- _is_consumable_type recognizes travel_stone
- _count_inventory_travel_stones helper counts stack quantities
- network_inspect action bar: slot 1 becomes "Buy (Stone x N)" when
  remote + stones held, "Need Travel Stone" hint otherwise
- display_market_network_inspect prompts the action-bar slot in copy
- _get_item_effect_description has a clear travel_stone description
- market_network_buy_confirm handler sends the message
- Changelog entry

[2026-05-15]  v0.9.454: Audit #11 Slice 9 — threat-corridor wandering monsters
Gives the Slice 6 "Under Threat" post marker mechanical bite. Standing
within THREAT_CORRIDOR_RADIUS (80 tiles) of an active T2+ world dungeon
now biases random encounters: the encounter spawns THAT dungeon's
monster_type at a level rolled in the dungeon's min/max range,
overriding the biome default.

Per-encounter cost is O(active_dungeons) which is ~60-70 entries on
the live world. Since trigger_encounter is event-driven (not per-frame),
this is well within budget. Picked the per-encounter scan over a
cached zone-map to keep the design dead simple — caching can be added
if encounter rates ever justify it.

Server (server.gd):
- New THREAT_CORRIDOR_RADIUS = 80 const.
- New _get_threat_zone_dungeon_at(x, y) helper. Same filters as
  _compute_post_threat_state (not completed, no owner, tier >= 2).
- trigger_encounter branches to the threat-zone path when the helper
  returns non-empty. Falls through to biome generator if the named
  monster isn't found in the DB (defensive).
- Monster dict gets threat_source + threat_color stamped so the client
  can flag the encounter visually.

shared/combat_manager.gd:
- get_combat_display passes through threat_source + threat_color so
  the client receives them at the top level of combat_state.

client/client.gd:
- _build_encounter_text appends "⚠ Spilled from [Dungeon] — you're
  in the threat corridor." line below the encounter banner when the
  flag fields are populated. Tells the player (a) the encounter is
  unusual, (b) which dungeon to clear, (c) directional hint.

Audit memo: Slice 6 explicitly named this as a future slice idea
("spawn raid mobs between dungeon and post"). User confirmed
direction: encounter bias today, visible roaming entities deferred
to a follow-on slice once perf budget is verified for dozens of
concurrent players.

[2026-05-15]  v0.9.453: Audit #6 Slice 13 — regenerating quest board (supersedes Slice 12)
Slice 12 (v0.9.452) misread the audit signal. "Daily stuff isn't
engaging" wasn't about kill-task CONTENT — it was about the daily-cap
mechanic ("do a couple of quests then wait until tomorrow"). User
clarified: "It's better to generate quests so they don't end."

This slice unblocks procedural quest generation (DYNAMIC_DAILIES_ENABLED
flipped back to true) and reworks the board into a sliding window:

- Board size bumped: 5-6 starter / 7-8 mid / 8-10 endgame (was 3-4/5-6/6-8).
- Completed quests advance the window floor; the slot fills with a new
  procedural quest at the next index. No cooldowns, no exhaustion.
- _generate_dynamic_quests walks indices from 0, skipping below the
  floor and over active+completed slots, until visible_count == board_size.
  Rng state advances on every index so _regenerate_daily_quest stays
  bit-identical for any single quest_id.
- _regenerate_daily_quest's burn-loop bumped to the new ranges so it
  stays in lockstep on the board-size rng call.

character.complete_quest now appends ALL turned-in quests to
completed_quests (was: dailies → daily_quest_cooldowns instead). Legacy
daily_quest_cooldowns kept as readable state so pre-Slice-13 entries
still fold into completed_at_post upstream.

MAX_ACTIVE_QUESTS dropped 5 → 3. With the board never running dry, the
incentive flips from hoarding to triage. Cap forces players to engage
with what they accept.

Client: [DAILY] tag dropped from row rendering — daily-cadence framing
retired. Featured tag relabelled "★ FEATURED ★" (was "★ TODAY'S BOUNTY ★").
Empty-state copy reverted from chain-only "try another post" message to
neutral "No quests available" (rare with regenerating board).

Memory: saved feedback_audit_signal_misread.md as durable lesson to
re-read literal user quotes before treating captured-decision actions
as load-bearing.

Server deploy required.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

[2026-05-15]  v0.9.450: Audit #9 Slice 3b — Exotic post rare-item access
Exotic posts now host a Curiosity Trader NPC with a daily-rotating
stock of rare items. Closes the audit's captured decision "exotic
posts → rare-item access" — gives the previously-unspecialised exotic
category a distinct vendor identity.

Pool (trading_post_database.gd): 10 curated items — Home Stones (4
variants), Mysterious Box, Boss Slayer Tonic, Reclaimer Lantern,
Floor Skip Charm, Elixir, Cursed Coin. Each has fixed price + supply
category. EXOTIC_SLOTS_PER_DAY = 4.

Daily rotation: hash(post_id, days_since_epoch) seeds an RNG that
shuffles the pool. Every visitor to the same exotic post sees the
same 4 items on a given day; tomorrow rolls a fresh subset.

resolve_post_category(post_dict, post_id) handles both procedural NPC
posts (carry "category" field directly) and legacy fixed posts (use
POST_CATEGORIES dict lookup). Slice 3 specialty code still uses the
old single-source helper — left untouched to keep this slice scoped,
but the procedural-vs-fixed gap is now documented.

Server handle_market_browse: prepends synthetic NPC listings to the
post's browse before the existing filter/stack pipeline. Synthetic
listings carry is_npc=true; the markup loop skips supply markup +
specialty discount on them (threat multiplier still applies). The
stacking pass also bypasses NPC listings so each daily slot stays
addressable by its synthetic listing_id on buy.

Server _handle_market_buy_exotic_npc: listing_ids starting with
"exotic_npc_" route here. Server re-derives today's stock for the
post and matches the listing_id; rejects mismatches (protects against
replay of stale listing_ids when stock rotates). Generates a fresh
item via drop_tables._generate_item at the player's level, charges
fixed price + threat multiplier, no listing removal, no sale history.

Client + market_panel.gd render purple [EXOTIC] tag on NPC listings.
Panel row font color shifts to exotic-purple for the same visual
signal.

Server deploy required.

[2026-05-14]  v0.9.443: Audit #11 Slice 7 — persistent NPC rumor cache + spam gate
Walking on/off an NPC post tile used to re-fire the greeting every
step (or stay silent for the whole session). Both extremes are bad UX.

Now: per-peer 60s cooldown gates the greeting (prevents step-spam);
per-account rumor cache holds for 30 minutes (re-visits and reconnects
on the same account see the SAME rumor — closes the audit memo's
'matches legacy trading post behavior' gap). Threats always run fresh
(stale dungeon-threat info is misleading) and invalidate any cached
non-threat rumor when active.

New: npc_post_greet_cooldown (peer_id -> {post_key -> last_ms}),
npc_post_rumor_cache (account_id -> {post_key -> {rumor, expires_ms}}).
Disconnect clears the per-peer cooldown but KEEPS the account-keyed
rumor cache so reconnects pick up the same narrative.

Old visited_npc_posts_session left in place for back-compat — the
real anti-spam gate is the new cooldown dict.

Three bugs from playtesting v0.9.434:

   lands in the else-branch isn't counted. _final_flock_kills now adds
   1 when flock_counts has an entry. A 4-wolf flock now correctly

   because they're parented to game_output_container.

   into _combat_loot_revealed_lines + _combat_loot_gear_drops and
   re-renders show_victory_card so the loot list grows in real time
   behind the panel. After Done, the card shows everything the player
   collected from the bag.

[2026-05-14]  v0.9.433: clan ranks / officers (Audit #14 Slice 4)
Leaders can promote any member to Officer (and demote them back).

retain full power — they can kick anyone (including officers) and are
the only role allowed to promote/demote. Unblocks the 'leader has to be
online for the clan to grow' problem.

  is_clan_leader / is_clan_officer / is_clan_leader_or_officer,

  officer entry. Member summary now includes is_officer + rank string.

  with handlers. handle_clan_invite loosened to leader-or-officer.
  _send_clan_info ships is_officer + officer_ids. _refresh_all_online_clan_members

  badges + buttons update without a re-open.

  when the viewer's rank allows the action against that target. Invite

  payload so the self-row check works.

[2026-05-13]  v0.9.428: combat Use Item picker overlays full panel — log strip was too small
Symptom: pressed Use Item in combat, saw the Use Item header + Prev/Next
buttons, but no items between them.

Root cause: the picker overlay was parented to _log_inner, which in the
Lufia layout is the small log strip at the bottom of the combat scene.
Title and action rows are fixed-height; the items ScrollContainer ended
up with ~0px of vertical room and the items rendered into a clipped
region (invisible).

Fix: parent the picker to the combat scene panel itself with a 24px inset
from the panel edges. Now overlays the full combat scene (player +
monster + strips all underneath). z_index=200 keeps it above the
battlefield overlay (z=100) and victory/death cards (z=150). The 24px
inset prevents a full-screen modal feel — player still sees the fight
border around the picker.

[2026-05-13]  v0.9.427: combat Use Item visible on first turn + server map-render perf
Client fix:
- Pressing Use Item on the first turn of a new combat now actually shows
  your usable items. There was a one-frame race between in_combat going
  true (_process_combat_start) and the combat scene panel's visibility
  sync (runs in _process before connection.poll). Pressing Use Item in
  that gap hit the `panel.visible == false` branch and rendered into
  game_output, which got hidden the next frame when the panel went
  visible. Picker overlay now fires whenever combat_scene_panel exists,
  not gated on .visible — its own visibility flag carries through.

Server perf (already deployed mid-session as server-only commits):
- handle_move was the top stutter source in the v0.9.426 diag (200-240ms).
  Fine-grained timing pinpointed LOS pre-compute as the cost: ~2640
  per-Bresenham-point chunk_manager.get_tile calls per render. Now
  pre-fetches blocks_los for the vision bounding box once (~529 fetches)
  and the LOS walker reads from a dict. Expected ~80% drop in LOS time.
- Also cached hotspot clusters per render (separate fix that did not move
  the needle but is real work avoided regardless).

[2026-05-13]  v0.9.423: ability content overhaul — auto-cycle hand, ability changes, balance pass
Hand mechanics:
- Every player action now cycles the entire hand: basic attack discards
  all 3 cards, ability use discards the played card + other 2, both
  paths draw 3 fresh cards next round. Solves the "stuck with
  uncastable cards" problem.

Ability changes:
- Cloak + Teleport: out-of-combat only. Refused in combat, removed from
  deck collections + equipped slots automatically (migration).
- All or Nothing: removed entirely. Action bar slot 0 (R) in combat is
  now empty.
- Tactical Retreat → Recharge: restores 50% of max primary resource,
  monster acts. Internal name "tactical_retreat" preserved.
- Haste → Arcane Surge: +40-60% spell damage + 7-25% double-cast
  chance on damage spells for 4 rounds (variable cost scales both).
  Damage portion uses add_buff("damage", ...) channel; double-cast
  tracked in combat state with per-round tickdown in monster turn.
  Magic Bolt, Blast, Meteor roll for double-cast at end of cast.
  Internal name "haste" preserved.
- Vanish → Phantom Strike: display rename only; internal name preserved.
- Banish: loot chance 50% → 70%.
- Pickpocket: per-fight cap roll 1-3 → 2-4; fallback 2 → 4.
- Forethought: simplified to "pay 1 resource, skip monster turn" since
  the auto-cycle handles the hand mulligan.

Client:
- Display names + descriptions updated for Recharge, Arcane Surge,
  Phantom Strike. All-or-Nothing references removed.
- Ability panel filters non_combat abilities (Cloak, Teleport).

[2026-05-13]  v0.9.417: universal combat pacing + Lufia-only layout + condensed-mode removed + strip-routing root cause + post-combat chrome order + clearer death
Big combat cleanup pass driven by user feedback during the v0.9.416 testfx pacing walkthrough.

Pacing (A in batch ABCD):
- Speed cycling (Instant/Fast/Normal/Slow) removed. Single set of delays: INTER_ATTACK_DELAY 0.78s (between consecutive attacks), POST_FINAL_ATTACK_DELAY 0.55s (after last attack), SEPARATOR_DELAY 0.6s, AMBIENT_DELAY 0.15s, END_ACTION_PHASE_GRACE 0.9s. No more per-tier branching in _drain_combat_queue.
- _speed_mult removed from combat_scene_panel.gd (18 multiplications); set_speed_mult() function deleted.
- _cycle_combat_speed(), _apply_combat_speed_to_panel(), combat_speed var, KEY_7 handler, Settings entry, keybinds save/load all removed.

Layout (B + D in batch ABCD):
- combat_layout is now const = LUFIA. LAYOUT_STANDARD / LAYOUT_CHRONO branches remain as dead code (never executes) but are slated for full removal.
- set_layout() function removed from combat_scene_panel.gd.
- /layout slash command + command_keywords entry + keybinds save/load + testfx force-LUFIA hack all removed from client.gd.

Condensed log (C in batch ABCD):
- condensed_combat_log force-disabled (saved value ignored on load, no longer saved, Settings entry removed, testfx firehose hack removed). Variable kept as no-op so dead branches compile.

Strip routing root cause:
- Replaced layered heuristic stack (BBCode-strip + verb whitelist + monster-name match + cyan-color blacklist) with a single structural discriminator: leading whitespace. process_monster_turn output gets indented 9 spaces via _indent_multiline in combat_manager.gd (lines 1130/1252/2610/2790/3670/3824); player ability side-effects emit via plain messages.append with no indent. Classifier uses that one signal.

Combat log ordering:
- Round 1 divider now appears (was: divider only fired from round 2 onward).
- combat_update divider emits state.round - 1 (server increments AFTER round resolves, so messages in first update with state.round=2 are actually round 1 actions).
- Damage totals + LOOT + Press-Space chrome now defers until queue drains (was: chrome printed via direct _combat_text_to_outputs while queue still had attack messages, so totals showed 0 and loot appeared before the kill).

Stuck-panel-on-Space:
- acknowledge_continue() now clears _pending_victory_fx_play, _pending_victory_card_payload, _pending_combat_end_chrome, flushes combat_msg_queue, resets combat_phase_timer, and force-calls end_action_phase() if still active. Previously the deferred end_action_phase_after timer could be bypassed by the early ack, leaving the overlay stuck visible after combat.

Server (combat_manager.gd):
- Always append "The X is defeated!" after any custom death_message flavor (line 1745). Custom flavor like "The troll stops regenerating. Finally." reads as poetry but doesn't clearly mark death; the generic line removes the ambiguity.

[2026-05-12]  v0.9.383: combat layouts third pass — chrono + lufia properly sized
User feedback on v0.9.382:
- chrono: player ASCII cut off on the right and midway down, monster
  ASCII covered completely
- lufia: same complaints, plus bars above the companion art instead
  of beside it (mismatching the Lufia II reference)

Root cause for both: scene_section didn't have enough vertical room
because (a) log_section took the same stretch_ratio as scene_section
and (b) the full 180×260 battle ASCII was bundled into the bottom
party blocks, inflating the row's minimum height to ~280px and
starving the monster.

This pass:

Refactor:
- Split _create_companion_block into atomic helpers:
  _create_companion_name_label / _create_companion_xp_row /
  _create_companion_hp_row / _create_companion_art_label. The same
  controls can now be arranged per-axis per layout.
- _companion_section type relaxed from VBoxContainer to Control so
  the Lufia HBox stat-box body can hold the same reference.

Chrono:
- scene_section stretch_ratio bumped 2.0 → 4.0
- Inner stretch monster:party = 4:1 (monster ~80%)
- Party row uses _build_compact_player_block / _build_compact_companion_block
  with portrait_size=72; player battle ASCII hidden (visible=false)
  to keep min height from inflating
- Bug-fix retained from v0.9.382: no CenterContainer wrapping the
  monster (collapses to min size)

Lufia:
- Same scene_section 4.0 / monster 3.0 / party_box_row 1.0 (~75/25)
- Each box is now HBox[ small sprite/portrait (72×72) | stats VBox ]
  per the SNES reference (was VBox[ name, portrait, art ] which
  bloated and put bars above art).
- Battle ASCII intentionally omitted from boxes (too tall for ~120px
  box height).
- Companion box: portrait on left, name + XP row + HP row on right.

Log section:
- stretch_ratio 1.0 in standard, 0.4 in vertical layouts so chrono/
  lufia actually get the vertical room. Log still visible, just
  tighter.

------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-12]  v0.9.382: bridge-restart bug + chrono fix + Lufia rewrite
Three issues addressed:

1. BRIDGE BUG (critical): _rebuild_all_player_enclosures (runs on every
   server startup) re-stamped every player tile into the chunk grid with
   `blocks_move = tile_type != "door"`. Bridges placed by players got
   re-stamped with blocks_move=true on every restart, silently breaking
   them. Mirror handle_build_place: `not in ["door", "bridge"]`. User's
   existing broken bridge will be walkable again after this restart.

2. CHRONO LAYOUT: monster ASCII collapsed to zero in v0.9.381. Root
   cause: wrapped _monster_col in a CenterContainer, which sizes its
   child to combined_minimum_size and ignores SIZE_EXPAND_FILL. Fix:
   place _monster_col directly in the VBox with SHRINK_CENTER horizontal
   (centering) and EXPAND_FILL vertical (lets stretch_ratio drive
   height). Same fix applied to Lufia (was about to make the same
   mistake).

3. LUFIA LAYOUT: v0.9.381's attempt was a side-view that didn't match
   the SNES reference. Rewrote per reference screenshots: monster
   centered at the top (~60% scene height), party row at the bottom as
   bordered stat boxes (PanelContainer with styled border), one box
   per member containing their portrait + name. New helpers:
   _build_lufia_party_box, _create_player_party_box_content,

[2026-05-12]  v0.9.379: bound road A* with a wall-clock time budget
v0.9.378 diag pinpointed the residual 5s freeze:

  FRAME SPIKE 4777.7ms (road_check=4776.2ms, ...)

_try_connect_road fires every 5 minutes and calls compute_path_between
in permissive mode with ASTAR_MAX_NODES=100000. On Hetzner CPX11 a hard
permissive search hits the cap → ~4.8s in a single frame.

Fix:
- compute_path_between gains an optional time_budget_ms parameter
  (default 0 = unbounded, preserves legacy callers). Inside the A*
  loop a wall-clock check fires every 256 expansions and bails to
  empty if the budget elapses.
- try_connect_one_pair caps total wall-clock at 200ms (sum across all
  edges in the iteration) and passes the remaining budget down to
  each compute_path_between call (so per-pair budget never exceeds
  the outer cap).
- connect_new_post (fires when a player builds a post) gets the same
  150ms budget. If pathfinding fails the player just doesn't get
  auto-road; the periodic check retries every 5 min.

Combined with v0.9.377's dungeon-spawn queue, both periodic causes of
the ~5s server freezes are now bounded.

[2026-05-12]  v0.9.377: dungeon spawn queue + granular spike diagnostics
Root cause of ~5s server freezes identified from diag logs over the past
week: _check_dungeon_spawns burst-spawned up to 8 world dungeons (BSP
floor generation + monster spawn) in a single _process tick, with
each spawn ~600ms on Hetzner CPX11 → ~5s frame spike per catch-up.
Spike pattern correlated exactly with the dungeon count jumps in logs
(56→64→80 at peers=0).

Fix:
- New _pending_dungeon_spawn_queue Array; _check_dungeon_spawns now
  enqueues up to 8 dungeon_type strings per 120s tick instead of
  spawning inline
- New _drain_pending_dungeon_spawn() called once per _process frame
  drains one queued entry through _create_world_dungeon
- Queue cleared on all three wipe paths (map / full / generic)
- Per-spawn timing logged when ≥50ms so we can see actual per-spawn
  cost on production

Granular diag breakdown (per-region timing):
- merchants / node_respawns / geo_events / chunk_save / drain_spawn /
  peer_io / buffer_process — usec accumulated and emitted in the
  FRAME SPIKE line so we can pinpoint the persistent 100-200ms
  baseline spikes that the dungeon fix doesn't address
- Spike logging rate-limited to one line per 2s window, keeping the
  worst spike of the window + count of suppressed ones, so logs
  stop spamming when constant spikes fire

DIAG_TIMING_ENABLED stays on one more release to verify the queue fix
hits the spike budget; then flip off.

[2026-05-11]  v0.9.350: sprite alignment actually fixed + no map shift at posts
Two related issues:

1. Sprite at NPC posts (5th attempt). Previous fixes v0.9.345-349 all
   failed because they read map_display.text — but in Godot 4 when
   content is added via append_text(), the .text property stays empty.
   find(" @") always returned -1, fallback math always ran. Switch to
   map_display.get_parsed_text() which returns the actual rendered
   character stream. The alpha-0 @ marker from v0.9.349 is now findable,
   get_paragraph_offset() returns real Y, sprite sits on the right row.

2. Map shifted ~2px on post entry/exit. The post header used [b] for
   the post name + "Under Threat". The bold font variant has slightly
   different line metrics, pushing the map block down. Removed [b] from
   post header (both NPC post and legacy trading post paths). Color
   alone differentiates the name.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-11]  v0.9.349: sprite alignment finally fixed at NPC posts
Root cause: the local-player @ glyph was being stripped from the BBCode
before append (line 27878 replaced "[color=#FFFF00] @[/color]" with two
spaces). The sprite-overlay code searches for " @" in map_display.text
to anchor itself via get_paragraph_offset() — but with the @ gone, find
returned -1 and the code fell to legacy math (2 + 11 + 0.5) × line_h.

That math is correct in wilderness (2 plain-text header paragraphs =
2 × line_h) but undershoots at NPC posts where [b] tags make the header
render taller than line_h. Result: sprite drawn ~1 tile north of where
the @ actually renders.

Fix: replace the @ with alpha=0 instead of stripping it. Glyph is
invisible but findable. find(" @") now returns the real byte position,
count("\n") gives the paragraph index, get_paragraph_offset() returns
the actual rendered Y. Works at wilderness AND posts because we're now
reading rendered geometry instead of doing arithmetic.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-11]  v0.9.348: movement responsiveness + lag spike spacing
- MOVE_COOLDOWN 0.5s→0.15s. Player was capped at 2 moves/sec. Felt like
  network lag but was a client-side input gate. The Oracle CPU spikes
  (fixed v0.9.344/346) had been masking this; once those were gone the
  cooldown became the felt latency.
- ROAD_CHECK_INTERVAL 60s→300s. Each successful road formation does
  A* (100k node cap) + path JSON write + dirty chunk save + merchant
  circuit recompute — 200-1000ms of sync work per spike. Spaced out
  5x and phase-staggered from dungeon spawn.
- DUNGEON_SPAWN_CHECK_INTERVAL 30s→120s. Halves the 8-per-tick spawn
  spike frequency. Refill within 2min is still acceptable.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-11]  v0.9.347: sprite alignment fix #3 — auto-detect line height
The "half-tile too high" sprite at NPC posts from v0.9.346 was caused by
the [b] post-name header. Bold text renders with slightly different line
metrics than regular text, so my uniform `line_h × idx` assumption
drifted by ~half a line height for paragraphs below the bold one.

Fix: instead of using `font.get_height()` as a fixed line_h, measure the
ACTUAL rendered line height of the @ row by subtracting its paragraph
offset from the next paragraph's offset. RichTextLabel knows the real
rendered spacing — we just ask it. This auto-corrects for bold, center,
font-size overrides, and any other layout effect that shifts metrics.

Remote players now also use the auto-detected line height for their
per-cell offset from the @ glyph (was using font.get_height — same drift
risk in extreme cases).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-11]  v0.9.346: throttle character saves + sprite alignment fix #2
Lag fix (server):
- save_character() now throttles writes to once per 3 seconds per peer.
  Each _safe_save runs 3 disk operations (read original → write .bak →
  write new) at ~100KB JSON each — on Oracle Free's 1 vCPU + slow disk,
  bursts of saves (combat-end + character_update + quest progress firing
  in succession) freeze the main thread for multiple seconds. The user
  reported "stops responding every so often, then catches up after 5s
  and moves all pending spaces" — that's the symptom.
- Throttled requests mark the peer dirty; _flush_pending_character_saves
  runs once per _process tick and writes at most one dirty character per
  tick, spreading disk I/O over many frames.
- Critical paths (disconnect, auto-save, death) call
  _save_character_immediate to bypass the throttle so nothing important
  gets lost.
- Throttle state cleaned up on disconnect so a future peer reusing the
  same ID doesn't inherit a stale cooldown.

Sprite alignment fix #2 (client):
- v0.9.345's BBCode-scan fix corrected the header-line count but reports
  of "sprite 3 tiles south of @" persisted on fresh logins at the starter
  post. Root cause was pixel math, not line count.
- Now uses RichTextLabel.get_paragraph_offset(at_line_idx) to get the
  actual rendered Y of the @ glyph's row, instead of multiplying the
  line index by font.get_height(). Picks up any [center] / [b] / line-
  spacing quirks the engine applies. Falls back to the math version if
  the API isn't available, and to the legacy header_lines=2 math if the
  @ isn't in the BBCode at all.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-11]  v0.9.345: hotfix — anchor map sprite Y to actual @ glyph position
Fixes the "sprite rendered N tiles south of where I actually am" bug
reported after re-login near player walls.

Previous code in _sync_map_sprites_overlay computed sprite Y as
  (header_lines + lcy + 0.5) * line_h
with header_lines hardcoded to 2. Every render path in
world_system.generate_map_display currently produces 2-line headers, so
the math was nominally correct — but the assumption was fragile, and
combinations of font-size override timing + RichTextLabel layout were
producing alignment drift in some sessions (notably first map render
after re-login).

New approach: scan map_display.text for the " @" glyph, count newlines
before it, and use that line index as the source of truth for sprite Y.
Local sprite sits exactly at the @ glyph's pixel Y; remote players
position relative to that anchor by their grid_y offset. Falls back to
the old math if the @ isn't found (defensive — happens in dungeon view
or before character load).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-05-11]  v0.9.344: hotfix — rate-limit dungeon spawns + memoize threat state
Two server-side fixes for the lag spike spotted after v0.9.343 went live:

1) Rate-limit world dungeon spawn batches
   _check_dungeon_spawns() previously spawned without bound until reaching
   MIN_WORLD_DUNGEONS (150) then optionally up to MAX_WORLD_DUNGEONS (200).
   When chunks become loadable (e.g., the first player connection after a
   restart), the spawner would burst-create 80+ dungeons in a single tick,
   freezing the server for ~1s and ballooning active_dungeons.size().
   New MAX_SPAWNS_PER_TICK = 8 caps each check; catch-up still completes
   over ~10 ticks (~5 minutes) with no design change visible to players.

2) Memoize _compute_post_threat_state per tick
   Slice 8 (v0.9.342) added a per-move scan that calls this function for
   every visible NPC post. With 150+ active world dungeons each call
   iterates the whole active_dungeons dict — handle_move + map renders +
   market handlers + healer/blacksmith encounters were all hitting it for
   the same post coords. Added _threat_state_cache keyed by "x,y" of the
   post, cleared at the start of every _process tick. Repeat lookups
   become dict reads.

No design changes. Same threat economy behavior.

dungeon within 80 tiles), every listing's markup_price is inflated by
POST_THREAT_PRICE_MULT (1.20). handle_market_buy applies the same multiplier so

[2026-05-11]  v0.9.327: Audit #12 Slice 1 — bubble radius scales with guard force
Player-post settler bubbles are no longer flat radius 25. An unguarded post
collapses to MIN_PLAYER_POST_BUBBLE_RADIUS=12 (marker zone). Each guard within
POST_INFLUENCE_RANGE=40 adds +2 radius; each tower-stationed guard adds +4.
Clamped to [12, 35]. Tier suppression now also counts guards in the same
40-tile range (was previously gated by the live bubble), so a guard just
outside a small bubble still contributes to its growth and suppression.

New helper _count_post_guard_force(cx, cy, owner) is shared by both
_compute_effective_post_radius and _compute_effective_post_tier so radius
and tier always reflect the same guard set.

Location message now ships region_bubble_radius / region_bubble_guards /
region_bubble_tower_guards. HUD renders one new line below the Outside warning:
"Bubble: r=N  guards=M (K towered)" — dim brown when guards=0, sage when
guarded. Players can finally see what their investment buys.

stacks, war banner damage buff, spectral veil monster-miss chance.

[2026-05-11]  v0.9.317: Audit #9 Slice 2b — buy-order delivery queue
Closes the v1 gap from v0.9.311. When a seller fulfills a buy order
while the buyer is offline or has a full inventory, items now land in
an account-level pending_market_deliveries queue instead of vanishing.

Queue drains on character_loaded: materials → crafting_materials,
inventory items → inventory slots. Anything that still won't fit gets
re-queued at the tail — items are never lost. Login receipt shows what
was delivered + any still-queued items.
